{"parentUuid":"dcd8242e-9093-4e05-9153-7cc6609ee115","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ad7f404dcc3b09202","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the I did **not** open `docs/missions/2026-08-06-v3-programming/reviews/s04-grok-rev1.md` (independence law).\n\n## Verification performed\n\n| Gate | Result |\n|---|---|\n| `pnpm run typecheck` | PASS |\n| `pnpm run lint` | PASS — `edgeRowsChecked: 27, violations: []`; `blocking: []` |\n| `pnpm test:s00` | **122/122 GREEN**, 23 files |\n| `pnpm exec vitest run tests/integration` | **34/34 GREEN on real embedded PostgreSQL 18.4** (reproduced independently, not taken on report) |\n| `pnpm run generate:contract` | generated contract byte-identical — in sync |\n| `web/` source | untouched (only `.next/` build output changed) |\n\n**Migration 0004 idempotency (round-1 regression):** genuinely fixed, not test-weakened. `packages/db/src/index.ts:27-35` still applies-then-registers each migration transactionally under `pg_advisory_xact_lock` and skips on the ledger row; `0004_s04.sql` is entirely `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` / `DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS … ADD CONSTRAINT`, replay-safe. The fixture change (`tests/integration/graph-database.test.ts:97-99`) widened a hard-coded expected list from four names to five and still asserts the exact ordered ledger contents plus a second `migrate()` resolving. Correct fix.\n\n**Seam A honesty (the S03 diamond's demand):** truthful. `packages/graph/src/index.ts:427` still returns `operatorResolutions: Object.freeze([])`, `resolveScoringOperator` still has zero production callers, and the deferral is a real generated row (`tools/orphan-audit/src/index.ts:158` → `reports/orphan-audit.json`) asserted at `tests/architecture/scaffold.test.ts:33`. No false attachment was claimed.\n\n**DR-115:** clean. Every judge call crosses `OpenAICompatibleProviderGateway`; the raw artifact persists unconditionally *before* the `response.ok` check (`packages/providers/src/index.ts:179-196`); failures write a real ledger row and never substitute an artifact; `judgementPolicy` is absent from `apps/runner/src/main.ts`, so production fails loudly with `JUDGEMENT_POLICY_UNRESOLVED` rather than composing a cell. All numeric fixtures are test-layer and labeled.\n\n## The three blocking findings\n\nThe ticket's ruled carriers did not all land, and the one honesty artifact the S03 diamond ordered kept current was not updated for S04's own unattached surface.\n\nCLAUDE REVIEW: CHANGES REQUESTED\n- SOLID: greenlight — boundaries, dependency edges and the single gateway hold; the duplicated claim-type rule is a non-blocking smell.\n- DDD: reject — two ruled DR-077/AC-92 carriers are absent, and the `disagreement` column is persisted carrying a dispersion reason.\n- TDD: greenlight — the RED is genuine (10 tests naming absent APIs before implementation), tests are behavioral, real PostgreSQL is used where DB-relevant.\n- Patterns (P-register): reject — P8's ruled canonical home for the composition map is unbuilt; P15's bulkhead has no production attachment and no honesty row.\n- DR-115: greenlight — no scaffolded data anywhere on the new surface; production fails loudly instead of composing a cell.\n- Findings:\n  1. **BLOCKING** — `migrations/0004_s04.sql` (whole file) / `packages/db/src/schema.ts:163-175`: **DM-3's ruled carrier did not land.** `07-build-order.md:291` assigns \"the claim-type → composition map's home at `register.register_row`\" to **S4** as an owned carrier \"that now need[s] an **owning slice and a DDL gate**\"; `07-build-order.md:772-776` and `09-traceability.md:519,736` repeat that the **structure is asserted at S4** at that canonical home. What exists is a TypeScript interface (`packages/judgement/src/s04.ts:158-172`) whose only supplier is a caller-passed literal in tests. There is no row key, no reader in `packages/register`, no DDL gate, and no test asserting the shape at `register.register_row`. The real obstacle — the key name and declared value type are `05-register-skeleton.md`'s and that row has not been minted there — is a legitimate blocker, but the handoff records **\"Questions for V: None added.\"** Either build the carrier + gate, or file the V question and record the deferral honestly; doing neither leaves an S4-owed carrier silently unbuilt.\n  2. **BLOCKING** — `migrations/0004_s04.sql:24-59`: **`ledger.node_strength_record.reduced_judgement_ref` is missing.** `02-data-model.md:1028` rules it under **DR-077** — S04's own entry ruling — with exactly this rationale: *\"without the ref, `strength` and `selected_judgement_ref` sit in two tables with no key between them and the ceremony cannot walk from a served number to the judgement that became it.\"* Its two sibling per-node columns from the same table (`operator_level`/DR-074, `lift_marker`/DR-072) both landed in `0003_s03.sql:12-15` in the slice that owned their ruling. 0004 added §6.3's half (`selected_judgement_ref`) and skipped §6.5's. Consequence: the DONE-WHEN's *\"demonstrated moving a serve\"* is demonstrated only at function level (`tests/unit/judgement-s04.test.ts:190-203`), never as a walkable ledger join.\n  3. **BLOCKING** — `tools/orphan-audit/src/index.ts:153-168` and `docs/.../S04-codex-handoff.md:95-101`: **S04's own unattached surface carries no honesty row.** `runJudgePanel`, `measureDispersion`, `applyCorrelatedErrorDiscount`, `applyDeclaredDisagreement`, `createTypedNonAnswer` and `resolveClaimType` have **zero production call sites** — only `reduceAssessment` and `selectReducedJudgement` are attached (`apps/runner/src/index.ts:178,187`). The ticket's soul (P15 bulkhead) therefore never runs in production, yet `neverCalled` gained no entry, the carry-forward section says nothing, and the handoff's inventory line 14 reads *\"real shell attachment: model assessment → deterministic reduction → DR-077 weighted selection\"* without the qualification. This is precisely the defect class the 12:46 S04 note ordered declared in `reports/orphan-audit.json`, *\"not only prose\"* — here it is not even in prose. Remedy is ~6 lines plus a `scaffold.test.ts` assertion.\n  4. NON-BLOCKING — `packages/judgement/src/s04.ts:324-327` / `tests/unit/judgement-s04.test.ts:205-214`: **FX-S22-01 is currently a tautology.** `applyDeclaredDisagreement` takes `fires: boolean` and returns the flag it was handed; `predicateRef`/`observationRef` are opaque non-empty strings. No predicate is evaluated over any observation, so nothing about the flag *firing* is demonstrated. The VR-1 note said authoring the case is design work *inside the fixture* with no invented threshold — and a non-inventing predicate was available (dispersion typed-`ABSENT` ⇒ the flag has no basis and cannot fire). Recommend either authoring that case or filing the V question.\n  5. NON-BLOCKING — `packages/judgement/src/s04.ts:278-290`: FX-LG-15 and AC-92 require the dispersion driver be **prepended** to the reducer's fixed driver order. `measureDispersion` returns a standalone `driver` object; nothing anywhere prepends it to `reduceAssessment`'s `drivers` array, and no test asserts prepending.\n  6. NON-BLOCKING — `packages/judgement/src/s04.ts:196,212`: `clarity = 1 − decay × ambiguityCount` and `uncertainty = fallacy.severity + decay × ambiguityCount` are undeclared source-literal compositions. The ladder rung they select is persisted as `uncertainty_ladder_position`, a frozen replay input, with register provenance for the coefficients but none for the formula that consumed them.\n  7. NON-BLOCKING — `packages/judgement/src/s04.ts:199-204,225`: a register cell with an empty `terms` array produces `tau = 0` — a manufactured score — and is stopped only by an untyped `Array.prototype.reduce` TypeError three statements later, not by a typed `COMPOSITION_UNRESOLVED`. Safe today by accident of statement order.\n  8. NON-BLOCKING — `packages/judgement/src/s04.ts:72-78` vs `packages/judgement/src/index.ts:89-95`: `resolveClaimType` is dead (no callers, not even tests) and **diverges** from `Judge.judge`'s inline duplicate of the same rule — an out-of-vocabulary model proposal is silently ignored in one and throws `JUDGE_SCHEMA_FAILURE` in the other. Delete one or make the other call it.\n  9. NON-BLOCKING — `packages/judgement/src/s04.ts:239`: the panel's primary member is typed `contractHash: null`, so any receipt built from `runJudgePanel`'s output carries no contract attestation for the primary (AC-92: *\"each member has its own judge role and contract hash\"*; P18). The runner sidesteps it by hardcoding `panelContractHashes: [judgeContractHash]` (`apps/runner/src/index.ts:235`).\n  10. ARRAY['branch', 'clarityDecayPerAmbiguity', 'terms', 'caps', 'uncertaintyLadder']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n      OR member ->> 'branch' NOT IN ('EVIDENCE_AWARE', 'EVIDENCE_FREE')\n      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'clarityDecayPerAmbiguity') <> 'number'\n      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'terms') <> 'array'\n      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'caps') <> 'array'\n      OR jsonb_typeof(member -> 'uncertaintyLadder') <> 'array'\n    THEN\n      RETURN false;\n    END IF;\n\n    FOR item IN SELECT value FROM jsonb_array_elements(member -> 'terms')\n    LOOP\n      IF jsonb_typeof(item) <> 'object'\n        OR NOT item ?& ARRAY['metric', 'coefficient']\n        OR item - ARRAY['metric', 'coefficient']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n        OR item ->> 'metric' NOT IN ('steelman_fidelity', 'counter_resilience', 'evidence_quality', 'evidence_relevance', 'context_fit', 'clarity', 'fallacy_resilience')\n        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'coefficient') <> 'number'\n      THEN\n        RETURN false;\n      END IF;\n    END LOOP;\n\n    FOR item IN SELECT value FROM jsonb_array_elements(member -> 'caps')\n    LOOP\n      IF jsonb_typeof(item) <> 'object'\n        OR NOT item ?& ARRAY['whenFatalType', 'to', 'why', 'by']\n        OR item - ARRAY['whenFatalType', 'to', 'why', 'by']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'whenFatalType') <> 'string'\n        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'to') <> 'number'\n        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'why') <> 'string'\n        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'by') <> 'string'\n      THEN\n        RETURN false;\n      END IF;\n    END LOOP;\n\n    FOR item IN SELECT value FROM jsonb_array_elements(member -> 'uncertaintyLadder')\n    LOOP\n      IF jsonb_typeof(item) <> 'object'\n        OR NOT item ?& ARRAY['atMost', 'label']\n        OR item - ARRAY['atMost', 'label']::text[] <> '{}'::jsonb\n        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'atMost') <> 'number'\n        OR jsonb_typeof(item -> 'label') <> 'string'\n      THEN\n        RETURN false;\n      END IF;\n    END LOOP;\n  END LOOP;\n  RETURN true;\nEXCEPTION WHEN others THEN\n  RETURN false;\nEND;\n$$;\n\nALTER TABLE register.register_row\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS register_row_claim_type_composition_map_shape,\n  ADD CONSTRAINT register_row_claim_type_composition_map_shape CHECK (\n    row_key <> 'claimTypeCompositionMap'\n    OR register.claim_type_composition_map_is_valid(value_json)\n  );","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"ee001865-6605-451c-baa3-aea0741cebd9","timestamp":"2026-08-08T12:03:39.494Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"29d616f3-737f-4879-87a1-b19588492476","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

 async executeWorkItem(workItemId: string): Promise<RunnerExecutionResult> {\n    return this.execute(workItemId);\n  }\n\n  private async execute(workItemId?: string): Promise<RunnerExecutionResult> {\n    const longestDeadline = Math.max(\n      this.settings.judgeBound.deadlineMs,\n      this.settings.composerBound.deadlineMs,\n      this.settings.conformanceBound.deadlineMs\n    );\n    assertClaimCoversCall({\n      claimMs: this.settings.claimMs,\n      deadlineMs: longestDeadline,\n      marginMs: this.settings.claimMarginMs\n    });\n    const judgementPolicy = this.settings.judgementPolicy;\n    if (judgementPolicy === undefined) {\n      throw new TypedDomainError(\n        \"JUDGEMENT_POLICY_UNRESOLVED\",\n        \"S04 requires V-ratified composition and judgement-selection register rows\"\n      );\n    }\n    const claimInput = { workerId: this.settings.workerId, claimSeconds: this.settings.claimMs / 1_000 };\n    const claimed = workItemId === undefined\n      ? await this.#work.claimNext(claimInput)\n      : await this.#work.claimById({ ...claimInput, workItemId });\n    if (claimed === null) return { kind: \"NO_WORK\" };\n    if (claimed.runId === null) throw new TypedDomainError(\"WORK_ITEM_WITHOUT_RUN\", claimed.workItemId);\n    const runnerAttemptId = randomUUID();\n    const run = await this.#runs.readFrozenHead(claimed.runId);\n\n    const completable = await this.#ledger.findSuccessfulCommandArtifact({\n      runId: run.runId,\n      workItemId: claimed.workItemId\n    });\n    if { readFile, readdir } from \"node:fs/promises\";\nimport { AsyncLocalStorage } from \"node:async_hooks\";\nimport type { Pool, PoolClient } from \"pg\";\nimport pg from \"pg\";\nimport { TypedDomainError, type ActivationState, type CompositionBudgetTier, type RiskTier, type TierSource } from \"@debateai/kernel\";\n\nconst { Pool: PgPool } = pg;\nconst writeTransaction = new AsyncLocalStorage<boolean>();\n\nexport function createPool(connectionString: string): Pool {\n  return new PgPool({ connectionString });\n}\n\nexport async function migrate(pool: Pool): Promise<void> {\n  const directory = new URL(\"../../../migrations/\", import.meta.url);\n  const migrations = (await readdir(directory)).filter((name) => /^\\d+.*\\.sql$/.test(name)).sort();\n  const client = await pool.connect();\n  try {\n    await client.query(\"BEGIN\");\n    await client.query(\"SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('debateai:schema-migrations', 0))\");\n    await client.query(`\n      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.debateai_schema_migration (\n        name text PRIMARY KEY CHECK (length(btrim(name)) > 0),\n        applied_at timestamptz NOT NULL\n      )\n    `);\n    for (const name of migrations) {\n      const applied = await client.query(\"SELECT 1 FROM public.debateai_schema_migration WHERE name=$1\", [name]);\n      if (applied.rowCount !== 0) continue;\n      await client.query(await readFile(new URL(name, directory), \"utf8\"));\n      await client.query(\n        \"INSERT INTO public.debateai_schema_migration (name, applied_at) VALUES ($1, statement_timestamp())\",\n        [name]\n      );\n    }\n    await client.query(\"COMMIT\");\n  } catch (error) {\n    await client.query(\"ROLLBACK\");\n    throw error;\n  } finally {\n    client.release();\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function withWriteTransaction<T>(\n  pool: Pool,\n  operation: (client: PoolClient) => Promise<T>\n): Promise<T> {\n  const client = await pool.connect();\n  try {\n    await client.query(\"BEGIN\");\n    const result = await writeTransaction.run(true, () => operation(client));\n    await client.query(\"COMMIT\");\n    return result;\n  } catch (error) {\n    await client.query(\"ROLLBACK\");\n    throw error;\n  } finally {\n    client.release();\n  }","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"75537310-2108-43f1-802b-4e73f2cd0d06","timestamp":"2026-08-08T12:06:22.120Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f2141f71-417d-4bbd-a44c-b0db318fc58f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

DR-077).\nALTER TABLE core.node\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS claim_type text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'unknown';\nALTER TABLE core.node\n  ALTER COLUMN claim_type DROP DEFAULT,\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS node_claim_type_closed,\n  ADD CONSTRAINT node_claim_type_closed CHECK (\n    claim_type IN ('empirical', 'causal', 'normative', 'definitional', 'prediction', 'comparative', 'mixed', 'unknown')\n  );\n\nALTER TABLE ledger.raw_artifact\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS parse_error text;\nALTER TABLE ledger.raw_artifact\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS raw_artifact_parse_status_check,\n  ADD CONSTRAINT raw_artifact_parse_status_check CHECK (\n    parse_status IN ('PARSED', 'UNPARSED', 'PARSE_FAILED', 'SCHEMA_FAILED')\n  ),\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS raw_artifact_parse_error_pair,\n  ADD CONSTRAINT raw_artifact_parse_error_pair CHECK (\n    (parse_status IN ('PARSED', 'UNPARSED') AND parse_error IS NULL)\n    OR (parse_status IN ('PARSE_FAILED', 'SCHEMA_FAILED') AND length(btrim(parse_error)) > 0)\n  );\n\nALTER TABLE ledger.reduced_judgement\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS uncertainty_ladder_position text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS uncertainty_drivers jsonb,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS score_caps jsonb,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS holes jsonb,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS branch_identifier text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS reducer_version text,","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"af7e7ad1-da93-44e6-830d-0748e0459971","timestamp":"2026-08-08T12:06:27.425Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"cb684c89-4490-4827-9046-ead8d5f8e714","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

The database gate enforces that `entries` must be an object, which aligns with the zod schema, so that's consistent. However, there's a gap between the database constraints and the zod validation: the database only checks that numeric fields like `clarityDecayPerAmbiguity` and `coefficient` are numbers, while zod enforces the [0,1] interval. Similarly, the database doesn't validate that string fields like `why` and `label` are non-empty after trimming, whereas zod does. S04 · Judge contract and panel (AC-04, AC-21, AC-92; DR-077).\n2\tALTER TABLE core.node\n3\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS claim_type text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'unknown';\n4\tALTER TABLE core.node\n5\t  ALTER COLUMN claim_type DROP DEFAULT,\n6\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS node_claim_type_closed,\n7\t  ADD CONSTRAINT node_claim_type_closed CHECK (\n8\t    claim_type IN ('empirical', 'causal', 'normative', 'definitional', 'prediction', 'comparative', 'mixed', 'unknown')\n9\t  );\n10\t\n11\tALTER TABLE ledger.raw_artifact\n12\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS parse_error text;\n13\tALTER TABLE ledger.raw_artifact\n14\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS raw_artifact_parse_status_check,\n15\t  ADD CONSTRAINT raw_artifact_parse_status_check CHECK (\n16\t    parse_status IN ('PARSED', 'UNPARSED', 'PARSE_FAILED', 'SCHEMA_FAILED')\n17\t  ),\n18\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS raw_artifact_parse_error_pair,\n19\t  ADD CONSTRAINT raw_artifact_parse_error_pair CHECK (\n20\t    (parse_status IN ('PARSED', 'UNPARSED') AND parse_error IS NULL)\n21\t    OR (parse_status IN ('PARSE_FAILED', 'SCHEMA_FAILED') AND length(btrim(parse_error)) > 0)\n22\t  );\n23\t\n24\tALTER TABLE ledger.reduced_judgement\n25\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS uncertainty_ladder_position text,\n26\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS uncertainty_drivers jsonb,\n27\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS score_caps jsonb,\n28\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS holes jsonb,\n29\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS branch_identifier text,\n30\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS reducer_version text,\n31\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS judge_weight_version text,\n32\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS selected_judgement_ref uuid REFERENCES ledger.raw_artifact(raw_artifact_id),\n33\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS dispersion double precision,\n34\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS panel_contract_hashes jsonb,\n35\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS disagreement jsonb;\n36\t\n37\tALTER TABLE ledger.reduced_judgement\n38\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS reduced_judgement_s04_complete,\n39\t  ADD CONSTRAINT reduced_judgement_s04_complete CHECK (\n40\t    (uncertainty_ladder_position IS NULL AND uncertainty_drivers IS NULL AND score_caps IS NULL\n41\t      AND holes IS NULL AND branch_identifier IS NULL AND reducer_version IS NULL\n42\t      AND judge_weight_version IS NULL AND selected_judgement_ref IS NULL\n43\t      AND panel_contract_hashes IS NULL AND disagreement IS NULL)\n44\t    OR\n45\t    (length(btrim(uncertainty_ladder_position)) > 0\n46\t      AND jsonb_typeof(uncertainty_drivers) = 'array'\n47\t      AND jsonb_typeof(score_caps) = 'array'\n48\t      AND jsonb_typeof(holes) = 'array'\n49\t      AND length(btrim(branch_identifier)) > 0\n50\t      AND length(btrim(reducer_version)) > 0\n51\t      AND length(btrim(judge_weight_version)) > 0\n52\t      AND selected_judgement_ref IS NOT NULL\n53\t      AND jsonb_typeof(panel_contract_hashes) = 'array'\n54\t      AND jsonb_typeof(disagreement) = 'object')\n55\t  ),\n56\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS reduced_judgement_dispersion_range,\n57\t  ADD CONSTRAINT reduced_judgement_dispersion_range CHECK (\n58\t    dispersion IS NULL OR (dispersion >= 0 AND dispersion <= 1)\n59\t  );\n60\t\n61\tALTER TABLE ledger.propagation_run\n62\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS judgement_selection_rule_key text,\n63\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS judgement_selection_rule_register_version bigint,\n64\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS judgement_selection_rule_source_ref text;\n65\tALTER TABLE ledger.propagation_run\n66\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS propagation_run_selection_rule_provenance,\n67\t  ADD CONSTRAINT propagation_run_selection_rule_provenance CHECK (\n68\t    (judgement_selection_rule_key IS NULL\n69\t      AND {"parentUuid":"4d966f54-31c8-44b1-a48f-7a6d488a828f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"ed809527-975b-406e-af04-cc3e6a68f6b6","agentId":"ad7f404dcc3b09202","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01ECoBEDsmFJf9n81R6xWXaK","type":"tool_result","content":"**Three additions the Theme C rulings force**, each the per-node half of a §6.4a\noutcome:\n\n| Column | Carries | Why it is here and not only at §6.4 | Ruling |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **`operator_level`** | the resolution level that supplied `operator_used` — parent, run or deployment | `operator_used` alone records *what* without *where from*, and DR-074's whole anti-defect property is that the operator is **a recorded config value, never a hardcoded literal**. A record naming the operator and not its supplying level cannot tell a register-resolved value from a source literal, which is exactly D2 | **DR-074** |\n| **`lift_marker`** *(shape fixed, not added)* | `{lift_kind, lift_target_node_id, marker_at_source, marker_at_target, applied_ordinal}` — the per-node projection of §6.4a's `lift_records`, **never a bare boolean** | manifest §4.3 names the field; DR-072 gives it content. A marker that says *\"a lift happened\"* and not *which* one or *to what* leaves both ends of DR-072's ruled marker pair unrecorded | **DR-072** |\n| **`reduced_judgement_ref`** | the `reduced_judgement` row (§6.3) whose selected judgement produced this τ | DR-077's selection is the join between the panel and the served number; without the ref, `strength` and `selected_judgement_ref` sit in two tables with no key between them and the ceremony cannot walk from a served number to the judgement that became it | **DR-077** |\n\n**No value is duplicated.** `dispersion` stays on `reduced_judgement` and the\nselection **rule** stays on `propagation_run`; this row carries the **level**,\nthe **markers** and the **ref** — three facts that are per node per run and are\nrecorded nowhere else (AC-85).\n\n### 6.6 `decision_record`","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"237ce5c6-cc02-4908-a0e2-bb01cbb4fb1e","timestamp":"2026-08-08T12:09:58.440Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4d966f54-31c8-44b1-a48f-7a6d488a828f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

Now I'm compiling the non-blocking findings for the next revision, looking at discrepancies between the migration SQL and the register TypeScript implementation.\n\nThe first issue is a type strength mismatch: the SQL DDL accepts looser constraints on numeric and string fields compared to the reader's Zod schema, which enforces unit intervals and non-blank trimmed strings. The divergence fails safely since the reader catches invalid rows, but having two separate declarations of the same rule invites drift—I should either tighten the SQL constraints or add a parity test to keep them in sync.\n\nThe second issue involves a CHECK constraint that calls a user-defined function, which creates a dump/restore ordering problem: `pg_dump` emits CHECK constraints inline in the CREATE TABLE statement, potentially before the function is defined, so rebuilding the database at this migration could fail. Additionally, later changes to the function via CREATE OR REPLACE would silently alter the constraint's meaning without triggering a new migration.\n\nThe third issue is hardcoded duplication: the claim-type keys and metric names are duplicated in the plpgsql validator, and while the existing test asserts membership against both migrations combined, a parity assertion scoped to just the current migration would catch any future drift—the pattern already exists elsewhere in the test suite.\n\nThe fourth issue involves collapsing two distinct validation failures into one condition, which makes it harder to distinguish between parsing errors and empty source references.\n\nThe fifth issue is that the production call site awaits the reader but never threads the result into the judgement policy, leaving it undefined; the only thing keeping the call alive is a textual assertion rather than actual data flow.\n\nFinding 6 involves a mismatch where the `neverCalled` reason claims the shell \"records typed absence,\" but the actual code persists a bare SQL `NULL` into the dispersion column instead—the typed reason exists in a sibling receipt, and the audit row correctly notes this but the wording is slightly imprecise.\n\nI'm verifying that the rev-1 findings (4, 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 12, 13, 15) are all documented in the handoff file under acknowledged non-blockers without claiming fixes, and checking the source code confirms nothing has changed: the disagreement consumption still happens, the dispersion driver isn't prepended, the clarity/uncertainty composition remains unchanged, the empty-terms edge persists, the panel primary still has a null contract hash, and the all-NULL degrade branch is untouched.\n\nI also confirmed that the 0004_s04.sql file's timestamp was updated during the rework but contains no new DDL—all post-review changes are correctly isolated in 0005, so existing databases will converge properly.\n\nNow looking at the per-axis assessment: the SOLID principle checks out, with the composition member type properly moved from the judgement package into its canonical owner in the register package, which means judgement now correctly depends on register.\n\nFor DDD, both ruled carriers landed as expected, the ubiquitous language gained a real term with `ClaimTypeCompositionMember`, and the disagreement column no longer carries a dispersion reason. TDD is also greenlit with fresh RED test cases recorded for both rework rounds.\n\nPatterns are approved — P8's canonical home is built at the ruled address with a DDL gate and reader, while P15's bulkhead is declared in the generated report. DR-115 is greenlit as well, with no value, cell, or seed in production; every numeric and policy fixture is test-layer and labeled, and production fails loudly at boot.\n\nThe verdict is approved.
